# Pocket Camera (Japan) — Disassembly Findings
# Work in progress, stay tuned

Primary target: **Pocket Camera (Japan) (Rev A)**, MD5 `fdcfe686cf4df461e870b6e53b2b5a8b`.
The international ROM is **not** a trivial derivative. Bank $0A, the sensor driver, differs from
the international ROM by only **61 bytes** out of 16384 (the exposure/dithering algorithm itself is
effectively region-independent), but the code banks $00–$09 differ by 8,874–14,932 bytes each: the
two ROMs are different builds that share the numbering of the modes and the flow of the states
(§16.1). The Zelda-edition ROM was not examined.

## Current status

![](/jp_rom_reachability_map.png)

*What the map shows:* the whole Japanese ROM drawn as 2bpp tiles (64 banks of 32 × 32 tiles; code, data and padding alike). Blue = executed or read by the v8 coverage run (dark: joypad and save only, light: only after a forced RAM or mode state), **red = never executed nor read and not identified** (4 bytes: 1 B of code, `00:1137`, the clamp of the D.J. envelope, + 3 B of data in `$1F`), **purple = code that never ran and is proven dead** (1,191 B, 0.1 %: dead functions that nothing calls and branches that no value can select, UNREFERENCED / UNREACHABLE, evidence I; sorted in §16.15, the attempts to force every piece with WRAM / SRAM pokes and the list of the desperate cases are in §16.21; the same pieces forced by a program-counter jump and a flipped branch and replayed in BGB, with the tile sets, maps and sounds they produce, are in §16.26), **green = never run or read by the v8 run, but a later targeted run did** (12,868 B, 1.2 %: **4,076 B of code**, 0.4 %: the SGB sender, the printer-only code and the printer error-status handlers in BGB, the hot-spot effect handlers, the hold-A stamp mirror, music-editor fields, the Game Face hidden choice, the RUN! RUN! RUN! podium and flag code, the Space Fever II boss code, the D.J. sound effects 4, 8 and 9, the two erase-all-photos shortcuts, the APU-gated branch, the link receive counters and the `$55` hello, the blank-calibration loader, D.J. page handlers, the copy badge of the editor save-back, the hidden Game Face editor and its save-back, the COMPOSE photo pickers, the slide-show repeat markers, and the cases forced by a WRAM / SRAM poke in my core (an armed hot spot, out-of-range cursors and effect indexes, printer size tables, a place counter, D.J. chords with A held; 421 B) or by a state number poked past the end of its table (137 B, not a designed path); §16.14, §16.18, §16.19, §16.21, `assets/witness_jp.csv`, C; **+ 8,792 B of data** that the v8 run never read and that later runs showed to be used: the **wild-frame lateral strips** (4,379 B, 0.4 %, of which 2,290 B are `$FF` runs that the padding rule would have hidden: the BGB blanking test changes exactly those tiles of the print, so the print path uses them, §16.13 (1), C on frames 02, 03, 05, 06, 07, 08), the **small-kana stamp pictures** `2A:6000-7E9F` (1,610 B: the stamp-menu walks of the display tracker put them on screen, median 264 frames, §16.24, C for the replays) and the **data sent to the Super Game Boy** (2,803 B: the border BGB shows in SGB mode equals the one rebuilt from them, §16.13 (5), C)), **orange = never read but identified as asset data** (2,372 B, 0.2 %, all evidence I: BG tilemaps 1,280 B, kana glyph and text tiles 679 B, SGB border tilemap blocks 199 B, tile-set gaps and tile data 214 B; §16.13, `assets/asset_map_jp.csv`), **yellow = the really unused assets** (21,422 B, 2.0 %, 34 spans, 12 of them TCRF-documented; evidence I: the spans of `tcrf_check/assets/unidentified/really_unused.csv` that no replay ever put on screen in a run without state pokes, §16.24: **the unused alternate stand-by screen of the image exchange `14:4000-502F` + `23:5CC0`** (4,724 B, §14.4), **the unused one-page main menu `25:7820` + `17:5500`** (5,440 B, §16.25), the TCRF-documented graphics (the hand flipping a newspaper `11:5800`, the kanji strip `0D:7660`, B CANCEL, the SOUND icon, …), the never-shown strips and tile maps; yellow takes 1,143 B from orange, 3,561 B from blue and 16,718 B from light blue, because most of these spans are read by a generic copier or by forced runs; the list is in §16.24 and in the section below the map, and nobody saw or heard any of it on a real camera), muted red = untouched padding (25,934 B). Generated by `tcrf_check/tools/rom_reach_map.py` from `coverage/v8/report_v8/rom_map.csv`, `assets/asset_map_jp.csv`, `assets/witness_jp.csv`, `assets/reach_ranges_jp.csv` and `tcrf_check/assets/unidentified/really_unused.csv` (§13, §15.1, §16.13, §16.14, §16.15, §16.16, §16.18, §16.21, §16.24). There is no equivalent run for the international ROM yet: its code is a different build (§16.1).

### What each byte is, bank by bank (the second map)

![What each byte of the Japanese ROM is, bank by bank: code by function, tile sets, tile maps, sprite layouts, sound data, tables, uncertain data and padding, with the tiles no run reached outlined](/jp_rom_layout_map.png)

*What the map shows:* the same 64 banks drawn as 2bpp tiles as above, but the colour is **what the bytes are**, not whether a run reached them; the shade inside a colour is the tile pixel. **Code** (117,627 B, 11.2 %: every byte of the static trace) takes the colour of the function of the routine that owns it: system 9,532 B (boot, interrupts, VRAM / OAM, blit, math, RNG, bank switching, SRAM storage, the SGB sender), camera 26,210 B (sensor driver, image processing, effects, the SHOOT modes and the parlor), album 11,341 B, editors 19,998 B, link cable 5,552 B, printer 8,343 B, mini-games 10,979 B, D.J. 14,181 B, menus 7,134 B, sound driver 4,323 B, and 34 B in no group. **Data** is coloured by kind, **even when no code calls it**: tile sets 669,767 B (63.9 %: 406,797 B screens, menus, fonts, sprites and the SGB border; 66,100 B stamps; 73,990 B print frames and photo borders; 122,880 B the 30 stock photos of Album B), tile maps 82,717 B (7.9 %), sprite layouts 24,780 B, sound data 12,466 B, other tables and text 59,655 B (stamp masks, the staff roll, SGB packets, the print layout scripts, jump and lookup tables), **uncertain 32 B**, and padding 81,532 B (7.8 %). A **magenta cross** marks every pure padding tile, i.e. a 16-byte tile whose 16 bytes are all padding (4,910 tiles, 78,560 B of the padding; shorter runs are not marked). An **outline** marks the 16-byte tiles that no run reached, with the classes of the first map: black = code that never ran (1,192 B), red = unused data (21,425 B), blue = identified data the v8 run never read (2,372 B); of the padding, 25,934 B were never touched. Per-bank byte counts of every colour: `assets/layout_by_bank_jp.csv`; generator `tcrf_check/tools/rom_layout_map.py` (shares `rom_reach_classes.py` with the first map).

*How a byte gets its colour:* code is a byte of the static trace (coverage classes `C`, `F`, `N` of `coverage/v8/report_v8/rom_map.csv`); its function group is the one of the label that owns it (nearest label at or before it in the bank, `disasm/sym_names_jp.csv`; the `ModeNN_` labels follow the mode table of §16.2). Everything else takes its kind from the name of its label: `Gfx_` / `Sheet_` / `Kana_` / `TileGap_` = tile set, `StampCat*_Gfx`, `WildFrame*` / `Border*` tiles and `AlbumB*_Tiles` / `_Thumb` = the stamp, frame and stock-photo tile sets, `Map_` / `BgMap_` / `*_Map` = tile map, `Song` / `Sfx` / `Snd` = sound, `OamList` / `OamPtrTbl` = sprite layouts, the rest of a code bank = tables. A `?` on a label doubts the role of the bytes, not their kind, so a `?` graphic stays a tile set. The 50 blocks named after the far pointer that loads them (`Src_*`, `Data_*`) get their kind from one evidence row each in `assets/layout_kind_overrides_jp.csv`. The kind is therefore as certain as the label or the row (C where a loader, a screenshot or a replay shows it, I where it is the reading of the bytes or of their neighbours, which is the case of most tile and map ranges that no code references). Padding is a run of 16+ `00` / `FF` that no typed range of `disasm/sym_types_jp.csv` covers, plus the zero fill the symbol file names `Bank*_Padding` / `ZeroFill`; forced runs read part of it (the 3,654 B of zero fill at the ends of banks $00-$02, for instance), so it is more than the 25,934 B nobody touched.

*The uncertain colour (32 B, magenta fill, not to be confused with the cross):* only the 32 bytes after the 12-row map at `19:7FE0` (no loader reaches them). The 7,520 B at `08:54DE-723D` are tables (C): the print progress packets and the five layout scripts of the printer job (§17.8). The other spans that were uncertain are classified by evidence (18,126 B): the 3 graphic spans tagged `?` (2,908 B: `13:5CA0`, `14:4554`, `1A:7C90`) are tile sets of doubtful role, and the 50 `Src_*` / `Data_*` blocks (15,218 B in banks $0C, $13, $14, $17, $19, $1C, $1D, $20, $21, $23, $25, $26 and $27) are 7,648 B of tile maps (C: the copy routine shows the BG-map destination: the animated background of the View menu, the main-menu slide strips, the stamp-category tile maps, the equaliser) and 7,570 B of tile sets (C where the copy shows a tile destination, as for the stamp-category icons loaded by `Stamp_LoadCategoryIcons`; I where the bytes render as digit, panel, picker or podium tiles). `disasm/sym_unconclusive_jp.md` lists the doubtful names with the test that would settle each.

*How the banks are organised (shares of the 16 KB of each bank; the first map's classes give the reach):*

| banks | what they hold |
|---|---|
| `$00` | the fixed bank: system code 32 %, tables 23 %, print code 17 % (printer driver and the print job), camera code 8 % |
| `$01`-`$02` | sprite layouts (OAM lists and pointer tables): 88 % of `$01`, 63 % of `$02`; `$02` also holds the storage manager (system code 23 %) |
| `$03`-`$09` | the mode code: editors (`$03` 73 %), album, editors and the parlor (`$04`), D.J. and BALL (`$05` 75 % + 15 %), the shooting modes (`$06` 78 %), main menu, link, Space Fever II and slide show (`$07`), print screens and the print layout scripts (`$08`: 49 % tables, of which 7,520 B are the layout scripts and progress packets at `08:54DE`, 30 % print code, 17 % padding), RUN! RUN! RUN!, the comment editor and keyboard, the user screens and the credits (`$09`) |
| `$0A` | the camera: sensor driver and effects, 68 % code, 17 % tables |
| `$0B` | the Super Game Boy data: border tiles 42 %, border map 10 %, packets and palettes 4 %, padding 44 % |
| `$0C`-`$22` | tile sets, 61-100 % of each bank (`$22`: 61 % tile sets, 39 % tile maps), except `$1F` |
| `$1F` | sound: 75 % song, effect and parameter data, 25 % sound driver code |
| `$23`-`$27` | tile maps, 72-88 % of each bank, the rest tile sets (9-25 %) and padding |
| `$28`-`$2A` | `$28` tile sets 78 % (padding 22 %); `$29` the staff-roll tables 46 % and padding 52 %; `$2A` the character glyphs and stamp sheets (tile sets 45 %) and the small-kana stamps (46 %) |
| `$2B`-`$30` | the stamp categories: stamp tiles 29-98 % of each bank and their 1bpp masks (tables) |
| `$31`-`$35` | print frames (`$31`-`$33`, 87-95 % frame tiles) and photo borders (`$34`-`$35`, 84 % tiles, 7 % maps) |
| `$36`-`$3D` | the 30 stock photos of Album B, 94 % picture tiles and thumbnails per bank, 5-6 % footers and tables |
| `$3E`-`$3F` | tile sets (88 % and 82 %), border tiles 9 % in `$3E`, tile maps 10 % in `$3F` |

*What nothing reaches, by bank (the outlines; bytes):* the never-run code sits in banks `$00` and `$02`-`$0A` and in the sound driver of `$1F`; the unused data sits in the tile and map banks `$0D`-`$26` (plus 3 B of `$1F`); banks `$27`-`$3F` hold nothing that no run reached except padding and 904 B of identified data the v8 run never read. ### The really never used assets (the yellow of the map)

![The REALLY unused assets on one sheet: the four unused tile maps each beside its tile set where one is known (the alternate stand-by screen of the image exchange, the one-page main menu, the photo screen with the hot-spot viewer's frame tiles), the TCRF-documented graphics, the never-shown spans, the scraps and the sounds nobody asked for](/tcrf_check/assets/unidentified/really_unused.png)

*The sheet* (`tcrf_check/assets/unidentified/really_unused.png`, built by `tcrf_check/tools/display_report.py`, regenerated 2026-10-14 after the L3 loader hunt; list in `really_unused.md` / `.csv`, sounds in `really_unused_sounds.csv`; method and every span in §16.24 and §16.25): a core that follows each ROM byte to the screen replayed 37,370 menu states and the stamp-menu walks; a graphic span counts as **never used** when none of its tiles was on screen (at least 15 frames) in a run without a state poke. The first block of the sheet shows each unused tile map right beside the tile set it is drawn with (`23:5CC0` with `14:4000-502F`, `25:7820` with `17:5500-67FF`, both drawn in BGB); the third, `25:75E0`, sits beside the frame tiles of the hot-spot viewer (`3F:7700-78EF`) with its photo cells hatched (a photo area is drawn with dynamic tiles, §16.25.2), and `26:7080`, for which no tile block of either ROM fits, shows an empty box. The verdict is **I**: nothing was seen or heard on a real camera, a tile composed by software would not have been seen, and "never on screen" is strong evidence, not proof.

**Today's count: 34 spans, 23,802 B** in 16 banks (30 graphic spans, 4 tile maps); 21,422 B of them are yellow on the map, the other 2,380 B are runs of 16+ `00` / `FF` that the map keeps as padding.

| tier (evidence I) | spans | bytes | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| no loader in either ROM (code search + BGB, `unused_manual.csv`) | 5 | 10,164 | the **unused alternate stand-by screen of the image exchange** (`14:4000-502F` + map `23:5CC0`, 4,724 B, §14.4) and the **unused one-page main menu** (map `25:7820` + tiles `17:5500`, 5,440 B, drawn in BGB, §16.25.1) |
| TCRF-documented graphics (`Unused_TCRF_*`, `tcrf_check/TCRF_CHECKLIST.md`) never on screen in an organic run | 12 | 6,656 | the hand flipping a newspaper `11:5800` (4,416 B), the kanji strip `0D:7660`, B CANCEL `1A:7D80`, the SOUND icon `10:7A80` / `10:7B80`, the circled X `15:4480` / `4500` / `4580`, the margin-print pieces `18:5A50` / `18:5F90`, the extra hot-spot icons `1C:5920`, the "cannot combine" text `1E:6620` (two more TCRF spans, `13:5CA0` and `1A:7C90`, are on screen in normal play and are not in the list) |
| never on screen, never read organically | 13 | 4,934 | 11 graphic spans (`0D:7000`, `15:5200`, `15:5500`, `18:7D80`, `1A:5182`, `1A:7E00`, `1C:5C20`, `1D:7B41`, `1E:7D00`, `21:6AC7`, `21:6B91`) and 2 tile maps (`25:75E0`, `26:7080`); read only by forced runs, no loader found |
| on screen only in forced runs | 4 | 2,048 | the frames 5-8 of the ten-frame camera ball head animation (`16:7380`, `7580`, `7780`, `7980`; the owner named them on 2026-10-14: assets of the head animation of the main camera menu, played on Up / Down): the shipped code plays frames 0-4 only, the second set is listed only by Tbl2 / Tbl3 of the page transition A (`07:7C33` / `07:7C3F`), and those two tables have no caller in the Japanese or the international ROM |
| **total** | **34** | **23,802** | |

*Sounds* (`really_unused_sounds.csv`, §16.24): 147 of the 162 sound ids were requested in an organic run, **15 were not**: 4 with no caller and no list (song `48`, `sfx1 24`, `sfx3 04`, `sfx3 07`), 3 in a picker list but never chosen (song `47`, `sfx1 2F`, `sfx3 0A`), 4 that share their data with a used id (song `08`, `sfx1 2B`, `3D`, `3E`) and 4 that have a static caller but were reached only by forced runs (songs `27`, `44`, `sfx4 05`, `11`; not proof of dead code). Nobody listened: the sound results are mailbox logs.

*Spans that look unused but are used by code* (they are in `unused_manual.csv` with the tier `used (withdrawn)` and are not in the count above). `10:74A3-753F` (157 B) is the tail of the numeral table `10:7400` that `DjTitle_LoadNumeralTile` (`05:423A`) indexes by digit (`$7400` + digit x 32 in bank `$10`, C from the code); it holds the tiles of the digits 5-9, which no replay put on that screen: value-gated, not unused. The eight spans `10:7602`, `10:78B8`, `10:7908`, `10:7A24`, `10:7ADE`, `10:7BF8`, `10:7D52` and `10:7F00` (2,089 B) are glyphs of the **printer text font** `10:7600-7FFF`: slot k is 16 bytes at `$7600` + 16 k, copied by `Gfx_Copy16FromBank10` (`00:1615`), which the D.J. score print renderer (`05:60EE`, calls `05:610D` / `05:611B`) feeds with the character codes of the 20-code row tables `05:5ABB-5EBE`. The rows are printed only to a Game Boy Printer, so no replay can put them on a screen: they are never *displayed*, not unused. They are labelled `Gfx_10_*_DjPrintFont_Tiles*` (C for the loader, I that every glyph of the span occurs in a printed row). No other yellow span lies inside a loader range (checked against every copy site with a constant length, the Parlor page lists and the numeral tables; `18:5A50`, `18:5F90`, `1C:5920` and `1D:7B41` are copied to VRAM by used screens but never shown, as the list says). The four frames `16:7380-7B7F` (2,048 B) are frames 5-8 of the ten-frame camera ball head animation (the owner's name, 2026-10-14); only Tbl2 / Tbl3 of the page transition A list them and those tables have no caller.

---

Everything below is graded by confidence:
- **Confirmed** — read directly from disassembled code and/or verified against raw ROM bytes.
- **Strong hypothesis** — code traced and consistent with the idea, but not independently cross-checked.
- **Open** — genuinely unresolved.

---

## 1. Toolchain, reassembly procedure and disassembly status

### 1.1 Toolchain and reassembly procedure

```
rgbds v1.0.4  (rgbasm/rgblink/rgbfix)  — installed, matches what mgbdis's Makefile expects
mgbdis v3.0   — github.com/mattcurrie/mgbdis, used for the static disassembly pass
```

Procedure (byte-exact, verified working). mgbdis v3.0 has no `--sym` option: it reads `<rom basename>.sym` from the folder of the ROM, so the symbol file must sit beside `pocketcamera_jp.gb` under that name:

```bash
cp pocketcamera_jp.sym /path/to/rom/folder/            # beside pocketcamera_jp.gb
python3 mgbdis.py --output-dir disasm_labeled pocketcamera_jp.gb
cd disasm_labeled
make            # runs rgbasm -> rgblink -> rgbfix -> md5sum
# md5sum must print fdcfe686cf4df461e870b6e53b2b5a8b
```

Optional readability pass (does not change a byte, checked by the same md5): `python3 mgbdis.py --print-hex --output-dir out pocketcamera_jp.gb && python3 tools/sym_fix.py out --tables --ram && (cd out && make)`. mgbdis v3.0 prints a label for *any* 16-bit operand equal to the address of a label of the current bank (or of bank 0 below `$4000`), whatever the operand means (`ld bc, Label` for a byte count, `ld [Label], a` for an MBC register write, a far pointer read in the wrong bank, a word table as `db $lo, $hi`). `tools/sym_fix.py` rewrites the cases that the code itself decides (74 MBC writes, 28 byte counts and 3 sprite positions in `bc`, 39 far-pointer loads, 1,762 + 274 word-table rows as `dw`, 260 `{lo,hi,bank}` rows as `dw Label` + `db BANK(Label)`); what is left of the artefact is cosmetic (a number printed as the label that happens to share its value). `--ram` is the L4 step: it writes every WRAM / HRAM operand with its name from `disasm/ram_names_jp.csv` and adds `disasm/ram_jp.inc` to the listing (§2.13); without it the listing keeps the raw `[$D5CE]`.

#### 1.1.1 The complete symbol file (`pocketcamera_jp.sym`, `disasm/sym_*_jp.csv`)

`pocketcamera_jp.sym` now covers **every byte of the 1 MiB ROM**: 5,243 labels and 7,467 lines in mgbdis v3.0 syntax (`BB:AAAA Name`, plus the block types `.code:LEN`, `.data:LEN[:WIDTH]`, `.text:LEN`, `.padding:LEN`).
Bytes by type: code 117,627 (the 60,449 instructions of the trace, so every `.code` block starts on a proven instruction and the disassembler agrees with the trace on every boundary), data 860,295 (with a row width per structure: 2 for word tables, 3 for `{lo,hi,bank}` far pointers, 32 for tile maps, 16 for tiles), padding 70,639 (1,042 B less than before the L3 pass: fill that sat inside tile tables and maps is typed with its table now), text 15 (`MAIN PASS` at `00:2E88` and the SRAM check string `Magic` at `02:4000`).
It is **generated, not hand-edited**: `tools/sym_build.py` builds it from two layered tables,
`disasm/sym_names_jp.csv` (`bank,addr,name,conf,note,source`: later layers win: assets < auto < hand < fixes) and `disasm/sym_types_jp.csv` (the block rows), and the trace `wram/trace_jp_v3.json`:

```bash
python3 tools/sym_build.py pocketcamera_jp.gb --names disasm/sym_names_jp.csv --types disasm/sym_types_jp.csv --trace wram/trace_jp_v3.json --out pocketcamera_jp.sym --report
```

To rename or retype something, edit a row of the two CSV files and rebuild; the builder refuses duplicate names, a label inside an instruction, a hole or overlap between blocks, and a `.padding` block that is not one repeated byte.

*Naming.* `Module_Verb` in PascalCase (`Album_DrawPhotoNumber`, `Cam_Calib_ValidityCheck`, `Sfx1_Init_3B_EraseAnim`); a data asset carries its type, bank, address and consumer: `Gfx_17_5500_Unused_MainMenuTiles` (2bpp tiles), `Map_24_4B20_PenMenuSizePanel0` (tile-map rows), `Src_BB_AAAA_Owner` (loaded through a far pointer, destination class unknown), `Sheet_<Name>_BB_AAAA` (a Spriters Resource sheet), `*_Part<addr>` (a continuation of the same asset). Each name has a confidence tag in the CSV: **C** confirmed (code read, README, emulator), **I** inferred (consumer or neighbour evidence), **?** doubtful: 3,790 / 1,448 / 5 (3,499 / 1,666 / 169 before the L3 pass of 2026-10-14).
*Where the names come from:* the 134 trace roots and the hand names of the earlier passes (README sections); the WRAM map (§2) for variable-driven names; the table-driven extents (message strips 15 x `$280` bytes, heads 24 x `$380`, the credits rows 234 x 32 bytes, the Album B slots, the stamp tables of `04:5337`, the pen-menu panel tables of `04:67A5`/`67EF`/`6839`); the TCRF and Spriters Resource sheets (`assets/spriter_sheet_map_jp.csv`, `assets/catalog_jp.csv`) for graphics; and, for 22 ranges, the picture of the bytes (`disasm/sym_unconclusive_banks/`).
A load site only counts as a far pointer when the immediate really reaches the banked helper unchanged: `ld hl,TABLE` + `add hl,bc` + `ld a,[hl+]` (a pointer table of the current bank, as in the pen-menu builders) is not one, while `ld hl,BASE` + `add hl,de` (a digit-indexed tile table) is.

*What is not settled* is listed in `disasm/sym_unconclusive_jp.md` / `.csv` (253 rows: every `?` name plus the `I` names of the groups below with their best guess, evidence and what would settle each): 102 mode-state handlers, 95 sound-effect tags, 31 dead-code names, 17 spans classified by content only (map rows vs tiles, named after the neighbouring consumer: `_Aux` / `_Unref`), 4 hand names not confirmed, 2 main-menu slide tables, 1 identified from the picture, 1 Run mini-game name. The 75 small unread data blocks, the 17 far-pointer assets of unknown extent, the 41 Run, 19 keyboard / glyph and 12 arrow names that the list held before the L3 pass are settled or reduced to the rows above (see *The L3 pass* below). All the graphic ones on one sheet: `tcrf_check/assets/unidentified/open_assets.png` (§16.23; a 2026-10-13 snapshot, 154 rows in the csv, most of which the L3 pass settled: the list above is the current one), and the really unused ones on `really_unused.png` (§16.24 and the section under the map at the top). Bank pictures of the unresolved data are in `disasm/sym_unconclusive_banks/` (`tools/bank_pics.py`; orange = span in the list, blue = other `?` asset).
Limits: names are inferred from code, README, WRAM map, TCRF, Spriters Resource and the pictures; they are not hardware-verified. Stamp storage (`2D:7000-7FFF`, noise-like) is open; the 96 effect slots of bank `$0A` and the 14 hot-spot helpers of bank `$03` are settled (§16.28).

*The L3 pass of 2026-10-14 and the effect-slot pass of 2026-10-18 (§16.28)* (names C / I / ? 3,499 / 1,666 / 169 -> 3,790 / 1,448 / 5; doubtful rows 610 -> 253; distinct doubtful bytes 50,221 -> 22,554, 2.2 % of the ROM; md5 round trip unchanged, also after `sym_fix.py --tables`; `08:54DE-723D` is typed as `Print_ProgressPackets` and the five `Print_Layout0` to `4` scripts, §17). Every change of a label is in `disasm/renamed_jp.csv` (584 rows against the start of the pass: 330 renames with old / new tag and the reason, 113 names kept with a better evidence tag, 116 labels removed, 25 new). What settled the rows, all C from the code unless a row says otherwise:
* **Loaders the copy-site scan does not see.** `Parlor_LoadPage` (`04:73EF`) walks lists of 7-byte records `{src, bank, dst, len}` (`Parlor_PageTbl`, `04:7436`: 7 records, `1D:6B00` / `7300`, `1C:4000` / `4800`, `21:5A00` / `6200` +`$800` each, `21:6560` +`$10`), which load eight spans of the SHOOT-parlor tile pages that were `?` (`_Aux` / `_Unref`); `SpaceFever_CopyTextRows` (`07:68D6`); the numeral tables indexed by value (`swap a`, x16 or x32: Album page numbers, D.J. title and tempo digits, hot-spot editor numbers, COMPOSE photo number, interval digits); and the **glyph font `2A:4000`**, 256 slots of 32 B read by `Kb_LoadGlyphList` (slot lists A / B / C at `09:53D3` / `53F5` / `5417`: `n, slot` = n glyphs, `$80+n, slot` = n copies) and by `Glyph_LoadFromCharCode` (`09:4802`, slot = char code - 1; base `$6000` for the small kana): the 17 "unread gaps" of the Spriters stamp sheet in bank `$2A` and the block `2A:59FF` are bytes of glyph slots that the coverage log shows read in full.
* **Fill-run splits hide extents.** A run of 16+ equal bytes becomes `.padding`, which cut tables and maps in two and left `Data_BB_AAAA` placeholders (75 of them) whose owner was the table around them; maps carry their `$FF` / `$FD` fill rows (columns 20-31 and rows after the copied span), counted with the map.
* **Names that embed a caller go stale** when function labels are refined: 115 asset names carried an owner from an earlier label set (`renamed_jp.csv`, reason "stale owner label").
* **False labels.** `09:6000` (`OwnerCard_LoadNameGlyphs_Gfx6000`) was made by `ld hl,$6000` with `C = $2A` (a pointer into bank `$2A`) and cut the last three entries off `Mode21_StateTable` (15 states = `09:5FE7-6005`): removed. A scan for the same shape (a bank put in a register, then `ld hl,imm` equal to an auto label of the current bank) found 23 sites; of the eight read by hand, seven (`06:5000`, `07:5340`, `09:5790` and four more) are real local tables that share their address with a far target, and `06:4008` is a sprite position (below). The auto labeller turns any executed 16-bit immediate that equals a ROM address into a data label, and `ld bc,$xxxx` is also how the code sets a **sprite position** for `Oam_AddMeta_*` (B = x offset, C = y offset). Reading the keyboard, slide, arrow and Run code found 19 false labels of that kind (`00:3780`, `00:3818`, `00:3878`, `00:3910`, `00:3D81`, `00:3E54`, `00:3F78`, `04:5018`, `05:5048`, `06:4008`, `06:7034`, `06:706C`, `08:5033`, `08:5833`, `08:5A59`, `08:6244`, `08:6259`, `08:6A44`, `09:6874`; six of them cut `Blit_ShiftTbl`, which is the whole of `00:3800-3FFF`, 8 pages of 256 bytes); they are removed (list in `renamed_jp.csv`, reason "sprite position of an Oam_AddMeta call"), the tables they cut are whole again, and `tools/sym_fix.py` knows the pattern now (`COORD_CALLEES`, `bc_coords()`: the operand is printed as `$XXYY` in the listing's coordinate form, not as a label).
* **Four groups read from the code (C unless a row says otherwise).** *Run mini game* (41 rows to 1): the player, rival and confetti sprite animations are lists of `{meta id, duration}` pairs run by `Run_DrawMeta` (`$FF, n` jumps to entry n), the line-up logic, the landing states, the finish-line x formula and the podium banner; `Run_ScaledPosition` was renamed `Run_OffscreenTest` (it tests whether a scaled position is off screen). *Keyboard and glyph* (19 to 0): the name-entry key pages, the cursor index to key position tables (`Kb_Page1..3_NavIndexToPosTbl`, read by `Kb_NavigateGeneric`), the voiced / semi-voiced mark maps `Kb_DakutenMapTbl` (key `$30`) and `Kb_HandakutenMapTbl` (key `$31`): records `{first code, count, new first code}` that move the character before the cursor to its dakuten or handakuten form. *Main-menu slide* (18 to 2): the strips are 10 frames A (`16:6980-7D7F`, 32 tiles each: the **camera ball head animation** in the centre of the main menu, the owner's name of 2026-10-14; renamed `Gfx_16_*_MainMenu_BallHeadAnim_F0-F9`) and 7 frames B (4 in bank `$16`, 3 in `$11`, 80 tiles each); `MainMenu_SlideA_Tbl2` / `Tbl3` (`07:7C33` / `07:7C3F`) have **no caller in the Japanese or the international ROM**, so the frames 5-8 of strip A (`16:7380-7B7F`) are listed only by dead tables (they join the really-unused list, §16.24). **Owner note (2026-10-14, 12:50): these four frames are assets of the camera ball head animation played when Up / Down is pressed on the main camera menu (SHOOT, VIEW, PLAY).** Checked in BGB (replay from the title: Start, then Down / Up / Left / Right on the upper page and the same on the lower page after Select, read watchpoints on all ten frames and on the four tables): only frames 0-4 are read (Tbl0 forward 6B80 / 6D80 / 6F80 / 7180, Tbl1 backward), never 5-8 and not 9; so the head that animates on Up / Down is made of frames 0-4, and 5-8 are its second set: the same 8 x 4 tile block with 10 or 11 of the 32 tiles changed (16 and 22-31, the lower rows) against frames 0-3, frame 9 being the resting frame of the lower page (`MainMenu_LoadIconTilesA`, `07:7827`). Evidence C for what they are and that nothing loads them in this ROM; the owner may have seen them play on a real camera or another ROM: if so the key path is not in my replays. Labels renamed, the four names went from I to C. *Arrows* (12 to 0): the arrow and cursor drawing routines (D.J. title arrows, hot-spot editor arrows, ...), each with its `Oam_AddMeta` meta list.
* **Loader hunt and read checks.** Two more loader kinds: **indexed font and record-list loaders** (the printer text font `10:7600-7FFF`, slot k = `$7600` + 16 k, read by `Gfx_Copy16FromBank10` with character codes from the row tables; `10:7A80` / `10:7B80`, the TCRF SOUND icon, is printed by `DjPrintRow_S2WaveTitle` when `$D985` = 1; the Shoot timer and digit tables `17:4000` / `4200` / `4820`, the comment-card digits `21:4D00` / `4E00`, `1E:6590`, `21:7BC0`, `23:7DC0` / `7E20`, the Parlor maps `23:7280` / `24:60A0` / `24:6140` / `24:62E0`). Two stray blocks were checked on the real BGB with **logging watchpoints** (a `-wp` read / execute watchpoint writes a line per hit and emulation goes on; a control watchpoint on bytes that certainly are read proves that a count of 0 means "not read"): `02:46E6` (3 bytes `00 C0 00` after the `jp` at `02:46E3` of `Photo_SaveReceived_Far`) on two linked BGB instances during a photo exchange (`tcrf_check/tools/bgb_link_wp.py`: the receiver executes the `jp` and never reads the 3 bytes), and `04:4D09` (18 bytes after the `ret` of `Mode0F_State09_EraseOne`, which read like 9 `{frame, duration}` pairs) on the replay of the corpus entry that erases one photo (`tcrf_check/tools/bgb_wp_probe.py`: the state ran twice, the 18 bytes were never read). Both are `Unused_*` labels (evidence I for the verdict, C for the BGB check).
What is left of the 5 `?`: the graphic span `14:4554` (the second half of the tile set of the unused image-exchange stand-by screen; its only reader is the thumbnail reducer, through a stray pointer, so only the picture or the owner can name it), the two TCRF candidates `13:5CA0` and `1A:7C90` (both lie in sheets that the album loaders copy whole to VRAM, the tiles have no copy elsewhere in the ROM, and the display tracker sees them on screen in organic runs, 22-26 frames and 65,535 frames: they match the TCRF pictures without being unused), and the two Space Fever II sound handlers of effect 24 (heard by the owner; no request of id `$24` in either ROM: the 334 call sites of `Snd_Sfx1` load 40 other ids, the one computed request `05:7B11` is `$20` + `[$D9E5]` and the six (id, channel) tables of `00:2A4B` hold no (24, channel 1)). The graphic spans `0C:5800` (23 of its 36 tiles are exact copies of tiles of the Animation-editor menu labels `0C:4D50`; no loader, no organic read) and `0D:7000` (fill and dither tiles; no loader in either ROM) stay `I`. The 253 doubtful rows are mostly generic names (mode states, sound effects) that need the owner or a debugger.

### 1.2 Disassembly status: what is proven, and what is lacking for a confident disassembly

**Byte identity is not the problem.** `mgbdis` + `rgbasm` already reassemble the whole ROM to the exact md5 above, because every byte it cannot type is emitted as `db`.
The open work is *confidence in the classification*: which bytes are code, which are tables / strings / tiles / song streams, and what to call them. That is measured with a
recursive-descent tracer, `tools/rom_trace.py` (v2: `--roots tools/extra_roots.json`; without `--roots` it reproduces the first version bit for bit; its symbol input is `tools/rom_trace_labels.sym`, the 183 labels of the first pass, because the tracer takes every non-`.data` name as a code root; `python3 -I tools/rom_trace.py pocketcamera_jp.gb tools/rom_trace_labels.sym OUT.json --roots tools/extra_roots.json` writes `wram/trace_jp_v3.json` exactly). A byte is *proven code* only if it lies on a decoded path.

| Trace | Proven instructions | Proven code bytes (banks 00, 02-0A, 1F) | Unresolved indirect jumps |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| first tracer (vectors, `.sym` labels, mode and state tables) | 56,799 | 111,808 | 14 |
| + 89 live roots and four rules (`jp hl` tables, callbacks, cooperative tasks, soft reset, OAM-DMA stub) | 59,866 | 116,613 | 0 |
| + 45 unreferenced "dead-code" roots (all 134 roots) | 60,449 | 117,627 | 0 |

The 11 code banks hold 180,224 bytes: **117,627 proven code (65 %)**, 1,256 inline jump / mode tables, 2,164 padding, 2,400 unreferenced (2,251 of them `0A:6BDD-74A7`: 1,827 zero bytes plus the popcount table `$7300` and the bit-reverse table `$7400`, both verified), and 56,777 bytes of data of unknown type.
The biggest single gain was the bank `$0A` jump table at `0A:541A` (96 entries, reached through `06:781D → 0A:7CFE`, the first tracer capped it at 32): +2,444 instructions of image-effect code. Indirect flow after the work: 31 `jp hl` (8 by table idiom, 23 by hand or rule), 46 inline `rst $18` tables, 276 far calls (72 distinct targets), 0 unresolved.
All consistency checks pass (no overlapping instructions, every callback ends in `ret`/`jp`, no invalid opcode on a proven path). The 45 dead-code roots decode cleanly but nothing calls them: they are *reachable by decoding*, not proven live.

**What is lacking, by importance**

1. **The data banks are unstructured.** *(Update, §13.5: the emulator run reads all 14 banks that no code references and logs which mode, state and routine reads which range; the type and extent of each asset inside a bank are still open.)* Banks `$0B-$3F` except the sound bank `$1F` (52 banks, 851,968 bytes) are not described: the asset catalog (`assets/catalog_jp.csv`, 385 copy sites) accounts for 254,546 bytes (29.9 %). 25 of these banks have no catalogued byte, and **14 banks (`2B 2D 2E 30-35 37 3A-3D`, 229,376 bytes) have no static reference at all** yet hold real content (not padding). They are reached through computed bank numbers and pointer tables that static tracing cannot follow. Until that is solved a source file can only `INCBIN` them, not name sections.
   Bank `$01` is not in these 52: it is the one data bank that is **already fully typed**. It holds the sprite-composition lists drawn by the OAM adders `00:24AF` (pointer table `$4000`, 249 entries) and `00:2496` (pointer table `$5D47`, 246 entries): each pointer leads to 4-byte OAM records (Y, X, tile, attribute) ended by `$80` (3,247 records in all; `A` = list number, `C`/`B` = Y/X offset added to every record, written to the OAM shadow at `$D400 + FF9A`). Parsing both tables covers `$4000-$7888` (14,473 bytes) with no gap; `$7889-$7FFF` (1,911 bytes) is zero padding. Bank `$02` has two more tables of the same kind (`$6E4B` for `00:2464`, `$5272` for `00:247D`), not parsed yet.
2. **About 57 KB of data inside the code banks has no known type or extent** (bank `$02` 11.9 KB, `$1F` sound data 12.3 KB, `$08` 10.0 KB (7,440 B of it are the five print layout scripts of `08:552E`, reached through the word table `00:32E8` and typed since), `$09` 5.3 KB, `$00` 4.1 KB, `$06` 3.4 KB, `$0A` 2.3 KB…): word tables vs strings vs tiles vs song streams. This is an upper bound: `tools/rom_coverage.py` credits a table as referenced when any `ld rr,nn` constant lands near it, and a bank-0 `ld hl,nn` ≥ `$4000` is credited to every bank. The only data of known extent is the 254,344 bytes copied through `call $0450` with a constant length.
3. **Reachable is not live.** 45 roots (1,014 bytes) are unreferenced code (library stubs, an unused sound-effect starter, twin routines `05:7F1C` / `09:721D`…). *(§13.3: in the emulator run none of the 45 executed from joypad input; one ran once in a forced run through an edge that is not in the static control flow, an artefact.)*
4. **Heuristic completeness.** The cooperative-task entries of bank 3 (program counters stored as split immediates `ld a,lo ; ld [$D6FD],a ; …`) and the 23 hand-resolved callbacks come from pattern scans, not from a proof that none is missing. The tracer also had one real bug, fixed in v2: an `rst $18` table at `05:48C4` was read one word too long and swallowed the first instruction of `Bank005_State12` as table data (bytes unchanged, labelling wrong).
5. **Charset table: only for the name / comment fields.** Strings other than the ASCII `MAIN PASS` (`00:2E88`) are not typed as text; the Japanese table of the photo name and comment fields is extracted since 2026-10-14 (`disasm/charset_jp.csv`, §16.27: glyph rule C, readings I), the other texts (menus, credits, printer font) are tile graphics and the international character set is not extracted.

**What needs the emulator or the hardware.** *Done:* the coverage run (§13; with the unlocking and link runs of §15.1, 57,582 of the 60,449 traced instructions (95.3 %) executed, 50,008 (82.7 %) from joypad input and an SRAM image alone, **0 executed instructions outside the trace**, none of the 45 dead roots executed from joypad input), the ROM-read log for the 14 unreferenced banks (§13.5), the link partner (§14), Super Game Boy (§16.13), the BGB re-trace of the covered paths (§17.7) and the never-run code (§16.14-§16.21, §16.26: 1,192 B left, all classified). *Still open:* what only hardware or a cycle-exact emulator can settle: the timing of link bytes, sensor-dependent decisions (§18.9), and anything seen or heard on a real camera.

**Choices left open for a rebuilt source:** the policy for the 45 dead roots (assemble as code tagged `unreferenced`, or leave as `db`; recommendation in §13.6), the character table for the texts other than the name / comment fields (§16.27), and a naming convention for the ~130 new code roots. Quirks that a rebuilt source must keep bit-exact are real ROM behaviour, e.g. `0A:4C51` is an unconditional `jr` that skips an `inc b` (it looks like a `jr z` that was meant, but the bytes are what they are).

Full report with the per-bank table, the resolved indirect sites and the verdict on the 15 unreferenced regions: `disasm/code_gaps.md` and `disasm/coverage_jp_v3.csv`. The runtime counterpart (what executes, what never does) is §13 and the package folder `coverage/`.

### 1.3 How far from a "full disassembly"? (measured 2026-10-14; the how-to for the next ROM is `HOWTO_NEW_ROM.md`)

"Full disassembly" means six different things (levels L0-L6 of the how-to). Measured from `pocketcamera_jp.sym` and from a listing regenerated with mgbdis v3.0 + rgbasm 1.0.4 (md5 `fdcfe686...` again, plain listing):

| Level | State | Measure |
|---|---|---|
| L0 byte-exact reassembly | **done** | md5 OK, also after `tools/sym_fix.py --tables` |
| L1 every byte typed | **done** | 2,224 blocks cover the 1 MiB: code 117,627 B, data 860,295 B, padding 70,639 B, text 15 B |
| L2 code proven and observed | **done** (dead-code verdicts are evidence I) | 60,449 instructions, 0 unresolved indirect jumps, 95.3 % executed, 1,192 B never run and classified (§16.21, §16.26) |
| L3 every routine / asset named | **mostly**: 0 `Call_` labels left, 5 `?` names (none of them the placeholder `Data_BB_AAAA` any more) and 1,448 `I` names | 5,243 names: C 3,790, I 1,448, ? 5 (start of the pass of 2026-10-14: 5,334 names, C 3,499, I 1,666, ? 169); the 253 rows of `disasm/sym_unconclusive_jp.csv` sit on 22,554 distinct ROM bytes (2.2 %), 197 of them generic mode-state / sound-effect names; every rename is in `disasm/renamed_jp.csv` |
| L4 RAM names and constants in the source | **names done, constants not** | 788 RAM names (C 674 / I 62 / U 47 / ? 5; `disasm/ram_names_jp.csv`, §2.13) are applied by `tools/sym_fix.py --ram`: 10,239 instructions use a RAM name (5,632 absolute loads and stores, 2,583 `ldh`, 1,926 address loads, 98 `ld hl, rIE`), the listing keeps no raw WRAM / HRAM operand except the 11 excluded numbers (`disasm/ram_exclude_jp.csv`), md5 `fdcfe686...` again. Not done: the constants (`MODE_*` for the values stored in `wMode`, the sound requests, the key masks are still numbers) and 8 accesses through a constant high byte (`ld h, $D3`) |
| L5 buildable, editable source tree | **not done** | no committed `.asm` (regenerated by mgbdis), no constants / enums / macros, assets not split into `INCBIN` files; 3,178 `jr_` local labels and 110 `Jump_` labels are mgbdis' own (normal for a disassembly, the 110 `Jump_` ones could be named) |
| L6 understanding | **partial** | a charset table only for the name / comment fields (`disasm/charset_jp.csv`, §16.27); the other texts and the international set are not extracted (the kana sheets of banks `$2D`, `$2E`, `$30`, §12), 14 banks with no static reference (§1.2), about 57 KB of data inside the code banks typed as data but without structure, the international ROM has no `.sym` (44 % of its bytes differ), the 45 dead roots and the purple code stay evidence I |

*Honest summary:* the ROM is **fully mapped and byte-exact**, and every instruction in it is proven and mostly observed; it is **not yet a decomp-grade source**. The RAM names are in the listing (§2.13). The next steps are the constants (`MODE_*`, sound requests, key masks), then the 5 `?` and the 253 doubtful rows (they need the owner or a debugger), then the text table, then L5 (split tree). The international ROM is a separate job of the same size as L1-L3.

---

## 2. WRAM / HRAM map (`$C000-$DFFF`, `$FE00-$FFFF`) — the consolidated, byte-level map

*Rebuilt from a recursive-descent trace of the ROM.*

### 2.0 Conventions, method and legend

* **Source of truth.** Every row comes from the instructions that a **recursive-descent tracer** (`tools/rom_trace.py`) proves reachable
  (56,799 instructions on proven paths, starting from the reset/interrupt vectors, the main loop `00:2E92`, the 34-entry mode table, every
  `rst $18` state table and the hand-labelled entries of the `.sym` file). `tools/wram_db.py` turns them into an access table: every `ld`/`ldh`
  that touches `$C000-$DFFF` or `$FE00-$FFFE` as **R**ead, **W**rite or **P**ointer load (`ld rr,ADDR`, i.e. the address is the base of an array or
  buffer). **1,135 distinct addresses** are touched (1,153 with the extended trace, see the re-check bullet below). A linear sweep of the ROM (what `mgbdis` prints) was *not* used as evidence: data decoded as
  code produces phantom addresses.
* **Blind spot, stated once.** Accesses made through a register pair that was loaded with the base address (`ld hl,ARRAY … inc [hl]`, `ld a,[hl+]`) show up only as a **P** row
  for the base. For arrays and buffers the extent, stride and role were therefore read from the code around the P rows and are written in the *Meaning* column.
  Targets of **data-driven callbacks** (bank 3 `$5F68`/`$5F91`, bank 7 `$792C`/`$7BF3`/`$7C62`/`$7CD1`, `call $FF80` = OAM-DMA routine in HRAM) are not followed by the tracer; the rows
  that depend on them say so.
* **Overlays.** Mode banks are overlays: only one mode is active at a time, and nothing clears `$D500-$D9FF` when a mode ends, so the same WRAM bytes
  have a different meaning in each mode (the *Banks* column lists every bank whose proven code touches the row). A row therefore describes **one use**; several
  rows may cover the same bytes (notably `$C000-$CFFF`).
* **Status** (same spirit as §3.0 for the SRAM): **C** = code-traced (writer and reader found, role evident from the code), **I** = inferred (consistent use, no instruction states the meaning),
  **U** = unused or dead (only written, only read, or touched only by unreachable code), **?** = inconclusive (the row says what is known and what is not). A name is never taken from a label.
* **Banks column.** Hex ROM banks (`00` = fixed bank, `0A` = bank `$0A`, `1F` = sound driver), `hw` = hardware register, `all` = used by every bank. Bank:address citations (`07:4A24`) are JP Rev A.
* **Re-check with the extended trace (§1.2).** The maps were written against the first trace. The extended trace (60,449 instructions: 134 hand-resolved roots, `tools/extra_roots.json`) adds **18 addresses and 520 live accesses**;
  all 18 addresses fall inside existing rows (`$C070 $C30E $C4F0 $C68E $C6FE $C770 $C7F0 $C90E $CA3E $CAF0 $CD0C $CD8E $CDFE $DA0D $DA0E $DA24 $DA29 $FF46`). The new live accesses are 479 in bank `0A`, 33 in bank 0 and 8 in bank 7.
  The bank-`0A` ones belong to the 96-entry jump table at `0A:541A` (image-effect routines of the SHOOT modes, reached from bank 6 through `06:781D → 0A:7CFE`, index byte from `06:60DB`, OR `$00/$20/$40` from `$D7EB`):
  they load start pointers into the photo area (`$C070 $C0F0 $C30E $C3C0 $C4F0 $C60E $C68E $C6FE $C770 $C7F0 $C90E $CA3E $CAF0 $CD0C $CD0E $CD8E $CDFE`) and use `$FF8A-$FF8D` as scratch (130+ accesses each), which agrees with the rows of those bytes.
  The bank-0 ones are the soft-reset path (`00:0210-028F`, already described in §2.2) and the bank-7 ones are the menu callbacks `07:7AF1-7B43` / `07:7BF4-7D30`, so the five rows `$DA1A $DA1F $DA24 $DA29 $DA2E` moved from I to C.
  `wram/wram_access.csv` and `wram_summary.csv` are the extended-trace tables; the per-row counts quoted in the region files come from the first trace and are therefore low by those amounts.
* **Where the full evidence is.** The tables below condense each row to about 150 characters. The un-condensed rows (with `bank:pc` of every key instruction), the per-region write-ups and the raw access tables are in
  `wram/` of the package: `wram_<region>.md` / `.csv` (7 regions), `wram_access.csv`, `wram_summary.csv`, `trace_jp.json`.

**Coverage of the accessed addresses** (mechanically checked on the extended table, 1,153 addresses: every address of `wram_summary.csv` lies inside at least one row; rows of a few bytes with a size cover arrays and buffers):

### 2.2 Boot, stack, OAM DMA, soft reset

* **Cold-boot clear** (`00:0181-0187`): `ld hl,$C000 / ld bc,$1FFF / call $043F` (`$043F` = store 0, `inc hl`, `dec bc`, repeat until BC=0) clears `$C000-$DFFE`; **`$DFFF` is not cleared on the cold path**. HRAM `$FF80-$FFFD` is cleared at `00:01A0-01A6` (`bc=$007E`); `$FFFE` and `$FFFF` are not. OAM shadow is initialised by `00:0895` (all `$F0`), BG maps by `00:087F`/`00:088A`, `$D520-$D522` zeroed at `00:01B6-01BC` (queue indices, see section 2).
* **Soft reset** (`00:0210` -> `00:0224-0246`): entered from the VBlank handler at `00:0317` (`push $0210 ; reti`) when `[$D523] != 0`, the held-buttons byte `$FFA1 == $0F` and `$FFA2 & $0F != 0` (`00:02F3-0319`). Joypad byte layout from `00:0A0E-0A33`: high nibble = d-pad, low nibble = A (bit 0), B (bit 1), Select (bit 2), Start (bit 3), active-high, so the condition is "A+B+Select+Start held, no d-pad, and a new press in the low nibble". It clears **all** `$C000-$DFFF` (`bc=$2000`, `00:023D-0243`) but **does not clear HRAM** (only `$FFB0-$FFB2` are zeroed at `00:0214-0218`, the DMA routine is re-copied at `00:0249`, `$FFC5` is zeroed at `00:025F`).
* **Stack**: `ld sp,$FFFE` (`00:0150`, `00:0210`, `00:0224`) is used only while WRAM is being cleared (the return address of `call $043F` then lives in HRAM `$FFFC-$FFFD`); `ld sp,$DFFF` at `00:019D` (cold) and `00:0246` (soft). So the stack is at the **top of WRAM, first push writes `$DFFE/$DFFD`**. There is no other `ld sp,nn`, `ld sp,hl` or `add sp,n` in proven code; SP is only *read* (`ld hl,sp+n` at `00:08D2`, `00:256B`, `03:5C5B`, `06:5424`, `06:54A5`, `06:5526`, `06:55A7`, `06:5624`, `06:566B`) and `ld [nn],sp` does not occur.
* **OAM DMA**: `00:03E2` copies the 10 bytes at ROM `$03F0` (`3E D4 / E0 46 / 3E 28 / 3D / 20 FD / C9` = `ld a,$D4 ; ldh [$FF46],a ; ld a,$28 ; dec a ; jr nz ; ret`) to HRAM `$FF80-$FF89` (called at `00:01A9` cold, `00:0249` soft). The VBlank handler calls it first (`00:02A7 call $FF80`) and then, in this order, `00:0AB9` (VRAM queue), `00:07EC` (HRAM word-copy request), `00:0868` (HRAM zero-fill request), then writes the LCD registers from the HRAM shadows `$FFAB...`.
* **For the HRAM map (§2.10)**: `$FF9A` = byte cursor into the OAM shadow (page `$D4`); `$FF9B` = ROM-bank shadow (every `ld [$2000],a` is paired with `ldh [$FF9B],a`, e.g. `00:1720-1729`, `00:08BB`); `$FFC7` is incremented once per VBlank (`00:031C-0320`), carry into `$FFC8`, and `$FFC9` free-running (`00:0328-032B`); `$FFCB-$FFCF` + `$FFD0` = VRAM word-copy request executed in VBlank by `00:07EC` (HL source = `$FFCB:$FFCC`, DE dest = `$FFCD:$FFCE`, count `$FFCF`, flag `$FFD0` cleared after use); `$FFD1-$FFD3` + `$FFD4` = zero-fill request executed by `00:0868` (address `$FFD1:$FFD2`, count `$FFD3` words, flag `$FFD4`).

### 2.3 The state machine: `$D5CE` (mode), `$D5CF` (state), and the 34-entry mode table

Every screen is a *mode* (`$D5CE`) made of *states* (`$D5CF`). The main loop (`00:2E92-2F37`, entered by the far-jump helper `call $08BB` from `00:020D` / `00:02A0`) runs the self-test far call (bank `0A:$6A52`), sets mode `$19` state 0
(title/logo), clears `$D5E0-$D600`, sets `$D523 := 1` (soft reset enabled) and then loops: `call $2F39` (mode dispatch) / `call $08A4` (OAM tail clear) / `call $0A82` / `rst $08` (wait for VBlank).
`$2F39` reads the 3-byte entry (lo, hi, bank) at ROM `$2F3F + 3·mode`; each mode bank starts with `ld a,[$D5CF] ; rst $18 ; dw state0, state1 …` (`rst $18` = inline jump table indexed by A).
The table has **34 entries (`$00-$21`)**; entry `$22` would decode as bank `$F7`, so the table ends at `$21` (verified on the ROM bytes).

| Mode | Entry | What it is | St |
|---|---|---|---|

A → mode `[07:7804 + item]` = `$01 $02 $07 $04 $06 $03 $05` for items 0..6 (`FF` refused); B → mode `$19` state 2; Start → mode `$08`. On screen: Japanese みる / とる / あそぶ, international VIEW / SHOOT / PLAY with the upper-left and upper-right items **mirrored** (§16.3); the lower page is the guide's *Studio Menu* (§16.2) | C |

### 2.4 `$C000-$D4FF` and the stack `$DE00-$DFFF`: work buffers, link buffer, print packet, VRAM queue, OAM shadow

**How to read this region.**
* `$C000-$CFFF` (4096 bytes) is *one big overlay area*. Its base meaning is the **photo-slot work buffer**: a RAM image of the first `$FB8` bytes of one album slot (photo `$C000-$CDFF`, thumbnail `$CE00-$CEFF`, tag `$CF00-$CF5B`, tag echo `$CF5C-$CFB7`). The same 4096 bytes are the **link-cable exchange buffer** (RX and TX base = `$C000`, `$1000` bytes). In nearly every mode it is also the staging copy from which the picture is uploaded to VRAM (or into which it is read back from VRAM); once uploaded, the WRAM copy is free and several banks reuse parts of it as tile/graphics/text scratch (section 3).
* `$D000-$D0BF` is small scratch (image-reduction accumulators, bank-7 collision masks, a bank-5 BG-row buffer). `$D200` is the print-animation packet, `$D300` the VBlank VRAM write queue, `$D400-$D49F` the **OAM shadow** (DMA source page `$D4`). `$DE00-$DFFE` is the stack.
* There is no `$C000-$D4FF` byte that is *never* touched by proven code except those listed as **U**.

**Evidence note (what the access table misses).** `the access table` records only accesses through absolute operands. Accesses made through a base register whose *high byte is a constant* (`ld h,$D3`, `ld h,$D4`, `ld b,$D4`, `ld d,$CE`) do not appear. I scanned every proven `ld h/d/b,imm`: hits are `00:0A8F`, `00:0AC2` (-> `$D3`, VRAM queue), `00:08AB`, `00:24CE`, `00:255B`, `04:530A` (-> `$D4`, OAM shadow), `02:45C3`, `02:47FE` (-> `$CE`, thumbnail). `ld de,$C0C0` at `03:6642` and `04:504B` is a pair of byte constants (D=E=`$C0`, tile-number bases) and **not** an address (false positive).

**Per-overlay summary of `$C000-$CFFF` / `$D000-$D0BF` (which bank puts what there).**

**Bank 0 (fixed code, runs on behalf of any mode).** The image reducers `00:2583` (thumbnail), `00:2656` (64x56), `00:2734` (quadrant to 4x4-tile grid) and `00:27F4` use `$D000-$D011` as scratch (a result pair written by `00:289C` and eight 16-bit accumulators). The software sprite-to-BG blit `00:1EA4` keeps a 5-byte-record patch list at `$C800-` that `00:213D` applies to a tile buffer.

**Bank 3 (slide-show editor and player, hotspot editor / viewer, cursor-choice screens).** `$C000-$C37F` holds a 64x56-px reduced preview made by `00:2656` from the photo; `$C000-$CDFF` is also edited in place by flash/transition effects before upload. A delay counter `[$D72B]` paces the preview reduction.

**Bank 4 (album / VIEW).** Four icon buffers: two tile buffers of 30 tiles at `$C000` and `$C1E0`, the icon source graphics at `$C3C0` and the blit mask at `$C5A0`.

**Bank 5 (modes `$1F`/`$20`, looks like a sound or note-sheet screen, **I**).** `$C000-$C058` is a packed settings preset, `$C000-$C1FF` stages the tiles of a 4-row level display, `$C200-$C47F` is a 160x16-px band (curve plot or text line, `$0280` bytes = one printer band), `$C480-$C493` a 20-tile text/notes source line, and `$D000-$D013` a 20-tile BG-row buffer.

**Bank 6 (SHOOT and related modes, plus the shared reducers).** `$C000-$C0FF` is the output of `00:2734` (4x4-tile grid), `$C000-$C1BF` the output of `00:27F4`, `$C000-$C1FF` is the two-photo blend rows, `$C080-$C0FF` quadrant composite pieces; `$C330`, `$C400`, `$CA00` are 16-byte effect regions and `$C700-$C7FF` is tile row 7 of the photo.

**Bank 7 (menus, frames, stamps, link).** `$CE00-$CEFF` is a motion-path table of (x,y) byte pairs and `$D000/$D040/$D080` are three 56-byte collision masks selected by `[$D866]`; the link transfers in `Bank007_State04/09/13/17` use the whole `$C000-$CFFF` as exchange buffer (section 2).

**Bank 8 (print).** `$C000-$C031` is an animation script (frame, duration) list; `$C000-$CEFF` holds the print page read back from VRAM for the printer packet builder.

**Bank 9 (scroll engine, thumbnail upload, RUN! RUN! RUN!).** `$C018-$C3D7` is a tilemap row buffer for the scroller and `$C000-$C017` hold animation scalars; the album thumbnail upload `09:45B0-45E8` reads `$CE00` (section 2). Mode `$21` keeps its race state at `$D000-$D092` (named in §2.13).

**Bank 0A (sensor, calibration, Tricks).** Sensor data from SRAM `$A100/$A800` is transformed into `$C000-$CFFF` photo rows, including the vertical mirror effects written through the second cursor `BC=$C60E` / `$CD0E`; `$C700-$C7FF` receives tile row 7.

### 2.5 `$D500-$D5FF`: animation pairs, VRAM queue indices, RNG, state vector, camera working set, mode / state, shared cursors

**`$D500-$D51F` sprite-animation pairs.** Slot n has a frame byte at `$D500+n` and a tick byte at `$D510+n`. A per-frame routine draws the sprite group of the current frame, increments the tick, and when the tick equals the duration stored with the frame in a ROM table it advances the frame and zeroes the tick (verified for the `$D50F/$D51F` pair at `04:7832-786E`; the other pairs use the same pattern, e.g. `03:649B`, `03:7117`, `03:7162`, `03:7774`, `07:55BA`, `07:620F`, `04:72E3`). `00:100B` clears the whole 32-byte area (callers `03:6371`, `07:558F`); other screens zero individual slots on entry. Which sprite each slot drives is only inferred (bank 3: cursor blink, sprite loops and two-actor scripts of the registration screens; bank 7: frame/stamp screens; bank 8: print animation, frame 0..4; bank 4: mode-1 cursor and the pointer-arrow `$D50F`). `$D509`, `$D50A`, `$D50E`, `$D516`, `$D51E` do not appear in the access table; `$D506` has no tick partner.

**`$D520-$D529` and the table.** Not a sound sequencer. The sound engine is bank `$1F` with its own state in `$DC00-$DC5E` and `$D9xx` (other regions). `$D520/$D521` are the write/read indices of the VBlank VRAM-transfer queue; the queue has only two writers (`08:4EE9`, `08:5290`), so most screens upload through direct copies (`$0450`, `$05F8`, `$0721`) instead. `$D524-$D526` are loaded with the constants of the next screen and faded in by `00:0D18`.

**Camera chain (bank 0A): how the 12-byte vector is used.** (1) Factory sweep `Cam_FactoryMeasure1` fills `$D59D-$D5B4` (12 REG4-bit results, 12 O-register results); `Cam_FactoryMeasure2` turns them into the 12-byte vector `$D5B5-$D5C0`, which `Cam_CommitVectorToSRAM` writes to SRAM (`$AFF2` bank 2 and `$BFF2` bank 8). (2) At normal start `Cam_Calib_ValidityCheck` loads the vector from SRAM, or the default `7E 7F 7F 7F 7E 7D 7E 7E 7D 7E 7D 6A`, into `$D5B5-$D5C0`. (3) Unless both SRAM copies are blank (`Cam_Calib_Loader`, §6), `Cam_Calib_BootMeasureSeq` (`0a:4724`) first calls `Cam_ExposureBandSelect` (`0a:4859`), which tries gains 0, 1, 2 (`$D587`), stores the winner in `$D588`, sets `$D589` (4 or 5) and copies three vector bytes into `$D5CB-$D5CD`; it then fills the sensor array `$A006-$A035` with each of five reference levels (`$D5CB`, `$D5CC`, `$D5CD`, `$D5BF`, `$D5C0`) in turn, runs the register search `0a:4B6A` and records REG4 & 7 in `$D5C1-$D5C5` and REG5 & $7F in `$D5C6-$D5CA`. (4) `Cam_MainDispatch` (auto-exposure step; A = `$D59A`) reads `$D5C1-$D5CA` and `$D587-$D589` per band, builds the dither matrix with `Cam_BuildDitherMatrix` (`$D583-$D586` from `0a:7C20`/`0a:7C60`) and updates `$D596/$D597` from the shift table `0a:7B00`.

**Photo-index family.** `$D5D8` is the album's current photo index (0..29, `$1E+n` stock pictures when `$D5DB` != 0). Bank-local copies: `$D5ED` (mode `$0A`), `$D5EE` (mode `$09` slider), `$D5F3` (print, bank 8), `$D5F6` (04 State05), `$D5F7`, `$D5F9` (via `$D671`), `$D5F8` (bank 3), `$D5FB` (bank 3), `$D5FC` (bank 7 random/slideshow). They are passed in `$FF9E` to bank-2 routines (`$5110` draw, `$4DD7`, `$4E31`, `$4C80`, `$4E5F`). Modes `$09` and `$0A` on 'A = yes' write `D5D8 := D5ED/D5EE`, `D5DF := 1`, `D5D6 := 2`, `D5D7 := 0`, `D5CE := $0F`, `D5CF := 5` (`04:6D28`, `04:7AC8`): this confirms the README §8 route into the erase viewer. `D5D6`/`D5D7` choose the prompt strip (`$8800`, 40 tiles) and the icon (`$8B00`, 16 tiles) of those pickers.

**`$D5D0` sub-states.** Seven dispatchers read it with `rst $18`: `04:4000` (3 sub-states `$400A` build/draw, `$40CF` browse, `$410D` confirm/exit), `07:4C08` (3), `07:699C` (4), `08:4DF2` (3), `08:5191` (3), `09:4281` (4), `09:7290` (3). Banks 3 and 6 only write 0 to it (`03:51F3`, `03:62F5`, `03:6329`, `03:6A24`, `03:6F9E`, `06:53F6`, `06:726A`).

**Bank 6 mode `$16` (option screens, `06:44D9`).** `D5E3` is the 4-item menu cursor; each item opens one of three sub-screens whose widgets are `D5E4/D5E5`, `D5E6/D5E7`, `D5E8/D5E9/D5EA`, plus a fourth screen with `D5EB`. All use two generic helpers: `06:4ABC` (vertical, HL = cursor byte, C = maximum) and `06:4ADA` (horizontal). The 'row' bytes `D5E4` and `D5E6` are called with maximum 0 so they never leave 0. On exit `06:4935` writes a selection code 3..`$15` to `D7E3` and sets mode `$14`. What the choices mean on the screen is not established (the strips are graphics in bank `$0D`).

**Bank 4 mode 1 (`04:6F96`).** `D5E0` and `D5E1` use the generic menu helper `04:720D` (DE = cursor byte, HL = table of (button mask, new index)); `04:7084` maps the chosen index to `(D5CE, D5CF)` = (`$15`,0), (`$01`,6), (`$16`,0), (`$09`,0), (`$01`,9). Selecting the `$09` entry goes through `04:7059`, which presets `D5EE := D561-1`.

**Cursor/choice bytes of other banks.** Bank 3: `D5EF` (mode 4), `D5F0`, `D5F1` (mode 5), `D5F2` (mode 6): Up/Down or Left/Right via `$FFA3`, bounded by compare constants, sound `$02` on a move. Bank 7: `D5EC` (mode 2, left/right), `D5FE` (mode 7, up/down). Bank 8: `D5FA` (2-way), `D5FF` (6-8 pages). Bank 9: `D5FD` (4-way, mode `$08`).

**Settings block and WRAM.** The 217-byte settings block (SRAM bank 0 `$B000-$B0D8`, flat `$01000`) has **no flat WRAM image**. Bank 2 moves it field by field: `$B000-$B02F` <- `$D681-$D6B0`, `$B030-$B05E` <- `$D6B2-$D6E0` (47 bytes each, `02:4A19-4A3C`), `$B05F/$B060` <- `$D6E2/$D6E3`, `$B061...` <- sound editor shadows `$D93D...` (`02:4A7D-`), `$B0BB-$B0D0` <-> `$DA96-$DAAB` (22 bytes, `02:5043`), owner block `$AFB8...` -> `$DA49-$DA5A` (`02:5054`). The only byte of it in `$D500-$D5FF` is `$D581` (= `$B0D1`, Game Face present). The other pieces are in §2.6-§2.8 and §2.11.

### 2.6 `$D600-$D7FF`: pen / stamp tools, hotspot and slide-show mirrors, SHOOT state

Only one *mode bank* is active at a time (`$D5CE` = mode, `$D5CF` = state, both dispatched with `rst $18` tables), and boot clears `$C000-$DFFE` once (00:0181-0187). Nothing clears `$D600-$D7FF` when a mode ends, so each mode initialises the bytes it uses in its first states and the range is reused as follows (banks 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 all touch it; banks 0, 9 and 0A do not write it):

| Sub-range | Bank / mode that uses it | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `$D600` | bank 6, mode `$17` | layout flag of the 4-photo / 2-photo composition screen |
| `$D602-$D614` | bank 4, modes `$10` (pen) and `$11` (stamp) | cursor position + velocity, pen option panel, stamp palette focus |
| `$D615-$D642` | bank 4, mode `$11` (stamp) | stamp category, per-category page / item memory, rotation timer |
| `$D643-$D660` | **shared, banks 2/3/4** | hotspot block of the current photo (mirror of SRAM slot bytes F36-F53) |
| `$D661-$D66C`, `$D66E-$D672` | bank 3 mode `$1A` (hotspot editor), bank 3 mode `$0C` (hotspot viewer), bank 7 save routine `07:699C` (with banks 3/4 as callers) | edit copies, cursors, pickers, save-dialog parameters |
| `$D66D` | banks 3, 4, 5 | shared "modified" flag (decides whether leaving an editor asks to save) |
| `$D673` | bank 3 mode `$12` | auto-fill option |
| `$D674-$D675` | banks 4, 6, 7, 8 | two choice cursors, a different meaning in each bank |
| `$D678-$D679` | bank 7 mode `$02` | animated-background frame / speed mask |
| `$D67A` | banks 4, 8 | "who launched this tool" code |
| `$D67B-$D67E` | bank 4 modes `$09` / `$0A` | photo-number slider |
| `$D680-$D6E3` | **shared, banks 2/3/4** | slide-show (animation) list mirror of SRAM 1000-1060 |
| `$D6E4-$D72D` | bank 3 mode `$12` | coroutine contexts, cursor and player state of the slide-show editor |
| `$D73D-$D7BC` | bank 3 mode `$12` | 16 spark sprites (position / velocity words) |
| `$D7BD-$D7C0` | bank 3 modes `$13` (`$D7BD`, `$D7BE`), `$05` (`$D7BF`), `$04` (`$D7C0`) | player pause / music, decoration-sprite X counter, blink hold |
| `$D7C1` | **shared, banks 2/3/4/8** | border (frame) number, mirror of SRAM slot byte F54 |
| `$D7C2-$D7D6` | bank 7 mode `$0D` | two wandering sprites; options and timer of the auto-play viewer |
| `$D7D7-$D7FF` | bank 6 modes `$14-$18` ( $D7D8-$D7DB: bank 4 mode `$01`) | SHOOT state: timer, options, burst list, compose screens |

**Buffers that mirror SRAM (all three are loaded / saved by bank 2).** (1) Hotspot block `$D643-$D660` = 30 bytes of the photo slot footer (slot bytes `F36-F53`): `02:4D88` (raw read, `HL=$AF36+slot`), `02:4DD7` (read + conversion of the 5 jump targets with `$15ED`), `02:4832` (raw write) and `02:488F` (write, conversion back with `$1600`); stock pictures (number >= `$1E`) come from the ROM table `02:5218`. (2) Slide-show data `$D680-$D6E3` = SRAM `1000-1060` (list 47 + loop flag, timing 47, speed, border), not per photo: `02:4EAE` / `02:4EE8` (load; the second converts the 47 entries slot -> photo number with `$15ED`), `02:49A8` / `02:4A07` (save, `$1600`). (3) Border `$D7C1` = slot byte `F54`: `02:4E31` (load, photo >= `$1E` -> default `$12`), `02:48F7` (save). Nothing else in this range is persistent. **Arrays indexed by album slot:** none live here (the per-slot tables are in `$D563+`, see §2.5); the only slot numbers in this range are *values* (list entries `$D681[]`, hotspot jump targets `$D65C[]`, `$D671`, `$D7EE/$D7F7/$D7F9` photo numbers). The two 20-byte arrays `$D615/$D629` are indexed by *stamp category*, not by photo.

**Bank 4, mode `$11` (04:56F9) = stamp tool.** State 2 (04:5733) initialises the cursor at (64,56) and loads the category graphics; the user moves a stamp cursor with the D-pad (04:5D92, about 1 px per frame), holding A for 3 s (180 frames), and then every 100 frames, runs a bit-reversal routine on the stamp bitmaps (04:5E4C-5FE1, timer `$D641`). **Confirmed in the emulator with joypad input only (§15.4):** the four work buffers change at each firing and are back to their original content after two firings, so the operation is an involution (a mirror-type flip), **not** a 90-degree rotation; a mirror of the stamp (the owner reports that holding A reverses it, §15.4); releasing A stamps it into the picture with the software blitter 00:1EA4 (04:5FE2; sets the dirty flag `$D66D`), Start or pushing against the picture edge for 10 frames opens the palette (state 3, 04:596D) in which Left/Right change page (`$D63E`), the D-pad moves in the stamp grid (`$D63F`) and moving onto the category column (`$D614`) lets Up/Down change category (`$D63D`, 17 categories; category 3 only if the CoroCoro flag `$D582` = 1). The last page and stamp of each category are remembered in `$D629[cat]` / `$D615[cat]` for the next visit (no other bank writes them, so they survive mode changes but not a reset). B leaves (state 5) or, when `$D66D` is set, goes through the bank-7 save dialog (states 6-8, `$D66E-$D671`).

**Bank 4, mode `$10` (04:604B) = pen tool.** Same skeleton (init 04:6089, drawing state 3 = 04:618D, option panel states 4-6, exit states 7-10) but a floating pen with inertia: 8.8 fixed-point position `$D602-$D605` and velocity `$D606-$D609`, updated by 04:6454 at a rate chosen by the speed option `$D60B`; A held draws the brush (blit data selected by size `$D60C` and pattern `$D60D`) at the cursor and sets `$D66D`. The option panel has three rows (size 4 choices, pattern 4, speed 3) navigated with `$D610` (row), `$D611` (focus), `$D612` (item), `$D613` (row shown).

**Bank 4, modes `$09` / `$0A` / `$0F` / `$01`.** Mode `$09` (04:683F) is the photo-option menu of the album: a photo-number slider (`$D67B-$D67E`, `$D5EE`) and four directional sub-menus: Left (04:6AC0, choice `$D674`: 0 = pen mode `$10`, 1 = stamp mode `$11`), Right (04:6B72, choice `$D675`: far calls 08:4DF2 / 09:4281), Up (04:6BF5: mode `$1C`, print) and Down (04:6C94: mode `$0F`, erase viewer); mode `$0A` (04:7488) is the same with the photo fixed (`$D5ED`). Before jumping to a tool they set `$D67A` to 1 (mode `$09`) or 2 (mode `$0A`) so the tool knows where to return (04:4C0B, 04:5CF8, 04:6389, 08:45B5). Mode `$01` (04:6F96, main menu) has three pages (states 0/2/4); each loads graphics and a tile-map pointer `$D7D9-$D7DB` for 00:0DA0.

**Bank 3 (modes `$12`, `$13`, `$1A`, `$0C`, `$04`, `$05`).** Mode `$12` (03:4000, 35 states) is the *slide-show (animation) editor*, not an owner-registration screen: it edits the 47-entry list `$D681` with timing / loop markers `$D6B2` (cursor `$D717/$D718`, Select menu `$D719`, range editing `$D71B-$D71E`), runs three cooperative drawing contexts (`$D6E4-$D714`, switched by 03:5649), previews the entry under the cursor after a short delay (`$D72B`) and draws spark sprites (`$D72C-$D7BC`); mode `$13` (03:5FA0) plays the show (`$D725-$D72A`, `$D7BD`, `$D7BE`). Mode `$1A` (03:69F5) edits the five hotspots of a photo (`$D643` block, working copies `$D661-$D664`, pointer `$D665-$D668`, number picker `$D669/$D66A`, dialogs `$D66B/$D66C`), mode `$0C` (03:6310) is the viewer that tests the pointer position against the hotspots. Dirty flag `$D66D` and the save-dialog parameters `$D66E-$D671` are shared with banks 4/5/7. Modes `$04` (03:7AA8) and `$05` (03:7BFD) use only `$D7C0` (cursor blink hold) and `$D7BF` (decoration sprite X).

**Bank 6, modes `$14-$18` (SHOOT).** `$D7DC-$D7FF` (+ `$D600`) are the working set of the shooting modes: `$14` (06:5DE6, live view / shot), `$15` (06:4000, self-timer and interval parameter screens), `$16` (06:44D9, option screens), `$17` (06:4C9F, photo composition), `$18` (06:598E, 4-photo list). `$D7E3` is the shooting *variant* (0 plain, 1 self-timer, 2 interval, 3-`$0B`, `$0C-$12`, `$13/$14` = 4-shot sequences, `$15`), `$D7DC-$D7E2` the countdown (frames / seconds / minutes derived from `$FFC9` deltas), `$D7E9-$D7EC` the four options of the option cross (shared with `$D674` as working copy), `$D7ED/$D7EE` the burst list, `$D7F3-$D7FF` cursor animation and photo pickers of the compose / list screens.

**Bank 7 (mode `$02`, mode `$0D`, save routine).** Mode `$02` (07:51AC) is a two-choice screen with a background tile-map animation (`$D678/$D679`, choice in `$D5EC`, next mode `$0A` or `$0B`). Mode `$0D` (07:6B03) is an *auto-play viewer*: a hub screen with two randomly wandering animated sprites (`$D7C2-$D7D1`, stepped by 07:6C10 / 6CCB / 6CF9), two options chosen with the working cursors `$D674` / `$D675` and committed to `$D7D2` / `$D7D3`, then a photo show with interval `$D7D4` (`$D7D5` countdown, `$D7D6` direction) that picks the next photo at random or in sequence (`$D5FC`, 07:7138). The save routine 07:699C takes its parameters from `$D66E-$D671` (shared with banks 3 and 4).

**Bank 8 (print).** Mode `$1C` (08:40F8) uses `$D674` as a 0/1 option cursor (08:452C, 08:4489), `$D67A` as return code (08:4128 / 08:45B5) and `$D7C1` as the border being picked (08:4F45-4FA0 Left/Right, 08:4EF9 shows `$D7C1+1` as two digits, 08:5037 loads it; a value `$12` is replaced by 0 and `$DBCC` := 1 at 08:4231-423E and 08:4825-4832).

### 2.7 `$D800-$D9FF`: print job, mode `$07` game, music editor / player, mini-games

The page is **not** one structure. It is a stack of unrelated overlays that happen to share the addresses:

| Range | What | Mode / bank |
|---|---|---|
| `$D800-$D816` | print job state (`$D801-$D814`), plus three unrelated bytes: `$D800` (launch flag), `$D815` (title scene), `$D816` (bank-3 sprite sweep) | bank 8 modes `$1B/$1C/$1D/$1E`, the margin consumer is in the fixed bank (`0:3339`) |
| `$D818-$D88F` | working set of **mode `$07`** (`7:54ED`): a vertical shoot-'em-up whose first wave is the menu that starts modes `$1F`, `$20`, `$21` | bank 7 |
| `$D890-$D9D6` | **the music ("sound") editor/player** (mode `$1F`) | bank 5 `$4000-$74CB`; playback engine in **bank 0 `$1017-$1560`** (called every frame from bank 5); RAM<->SRAM pack/unpack in **bank 2 `$4A6B` / `$4F2B`** |
| `$D9D7-$D9F7` | mode `$20` (`5:74CC`): a small three-object action game with its own two-row menu | bank 5 `$74CC-$7FFF` |
| `$D9F8-$D9F9` | character pick of mode `$21` (`9:5FE3`) | bank 9 |
| `$D9FA-$D9FF` | four sprite animators of mode `$0E` (`7:4000`, link-cable exchange screen) | bank 7 |

(The Game Boy Camera documentation names its games "Space Fever II", "Ball", "Run! Run! Run!" and the music editor "DJ"; matching them to modes `$07`, `$20`, `$21`, `$1F` is an inference from behaviour, not from text found in the ROM, and is tagged I wherever it appears. The code facts do not depend on it.)

The three 3-byte cursors of `$D890-$D9F8` are the *field cursors of the three channel pages* (SOUND I, SOUND II, NOISE) of the sound editor; `$D9D1` is the **tempo**, `$D9D2` the "sound saved" flag, `$D9D3/$D9D5/$D9D6` menu selections of the sound editor's start menu.

**The 217-byte settings block is NOT mirrored in WRAM as one block.** The sound part of the SRAM settings block (`$1061-$10BA`, 90 bytes) is *unpacked* into a byte-per-field layout at `$D93D-$D9D2`; the animation list (`$1000-$105E`) is at `$D681-$D6E3` (other region); a flat 22-byte image of `$10BB-$10D0` sits at `$DA96-$DAAB` (other region); the Game Face flag `$10D1` is `$D581`. The base of the sound image and the full field-by-field map are in section 3.1.

**SRAM settings block <-> WRAM (sound editor).**

The README §3.2 block is **217 bytes = 215 data bytes `$1000-$10D6` + 2 checksum bytes, echoed at +`$D9`**: the writer `2:4BB2-4BF1` saves two copies (`DE = 0` and `DE = $D9`, `B = 2`) and calls the checksum routine `2:432F` with `HL = $B000 + DE`, `BC = $00D7`. Bank 2 never moves it as one block: each part has its own WRAM image.

| SRAM offset (flat) | Size | WRAM image | Format in WRAM | Moved by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `$1000-$105E` | 47 | `$D681-$D6E3` (animation list, not this page) | see `wram_d600.md` | other region |
| `$1061` | 1 | `$D93D` duty (bits 5-4), `$D93E` length index (bits 3-0) | 1 byte per field | unpack `2:4F32`, pack `2:4A81` |
| `$1062` | 1 | `$D93F` U/D (bit 7), `$D940` period (bits 6-4), `$D941` volume (bits 3-0) | 1 byte per field | `2:4F38` / `2:4A87` |
| `$1063-$1064` | 2 | `$D942` depth, `$D943` speed (low 7 bits), `$D944` mode (bit 7 of `$1063` = bit 0, bit 7 of `$1064` = bit 1) | | `2:4F3E` / `2:4A8D` |
| `$1065-$1074` | 16 | `$D945-$D954` SOUND I notes | 1 byte per step | `2:4F44` / `2:4A93` |
| `$1075-$1078` | 4 | `$D955-$D964` SOUND I stereo codes | 2 bits per step, MSB first | `2:4F4A` / `2:4A99` |
| `$1079-$1088` | 16 | `$D965-$D984` user wave | 1 nibble per byte, high nibble first | `2:4F50` / `2:4A9F` |
| `$1089` | 1 | `$D985` wave pattern (bits 5-4), `$D986` length index (bits 3-0) | | `2:4F56` / `2:4AA5` |
| `$108A-$108B` | 2 | `$D987-$D989` SOUND II modulation depth, speed, mode | as `$1063-$1064` | `2:4F5C` / `2:4AAB` |
| `$108C-$109B` | 16 | `$D98A-$D999` SOUND II notes | | `2:4F62` / `2:4AB1` |
| `$109C-$109F` | 4 | `$D99A-$D9A9` SOUND II stereo codes | 2 bits per step | `2:4F68` / `2:4AB7` |
| `$10A0` | 1 | `$D9AA` (high nibble + 1), `$D9AB` (low nibble + 1) = loop lengths SOUND I / II | | `2:4F6E` / `2:4ABD` |
| `$10A1` | 1 | `$D9AC` U/D, `$D9AD` period, `$D9AE` volume (NOISE) | as `$1062` | `2:4F7E` / `2:4ACE` |
| `$10A2` | 1 | `$D9AF` gate (high nibble), `$D9B0` loop length (low nibble + 1) | | `2:4F84` / `2:4AD4` |
| `$10A3-$10B2` | 16 | `$D9B1-$D9C0` NOISE notes | | `2:4F93` / `2:4AE4` |
| `$10B3-$10B6` | 4 | `$D9C1-$D9D0` bits 1-0 = stereo code | 2 bits per step | `2:4F99` / pack `2:4AEA` |
| `$10B7-$10B8` | 2 | `$D9C1-$D9D0` bit 4 = width flag | 1 bit per step, MSB = step 1 | `2:5028` / `2:4AF0` |
| `$10B9` | 1 | `$D9D1` tempo | byte | `2:4FA6` / `2:4AF6` |
| `$10BA` | 1 | `$D9D2` "saved tune present" flag | byte | `2:4FAA` |
| `$10BB-$10D0` | 22 | `$DA96-$DAAB` (outside this page): flat 22-byte image saved by `2:4BB2`; contains the two best scores `$DAA0-$DAA3` (shooter, SRAM `$10C5-$10C8`) and `$DAA4-$DAA5` (mode `$20`, SRAM `$10C9-$10CA`) | flat | `2:4BC4-4BD1` |
| `$10D1` | 1 | `$D581` Game Face present flag (bank 2 `02:506A`) | byte | other region |

So `$1061-$10BA` (90 bytes) is unpacked into the 150 bytes `$D93D-$D9D2`. The base of the sound-settings image is `$D93D`; there is no WRAM address from which `$1000-$10D8` can be read as one block.

### 2.8 `$DA00-$DBFF`: link screen, main menu, owner / tag / statistics mirrors, delete effect, print engine

The range is **not one structure**: it is a stack of unrelated overlays plus two SRAM mirrors, a statistics block and the print engine state. Mode banks are overlays, so the same page is reused by different banks with different meanings (listed per address in the tables).

| Range | What | Bank / mode |
|---|---|---|
| `$DA00-$DA0C`, `$DBCE` | link-cable exchange screen: cable characters, transfer animation, photo-pick dialog, time-out counter | bank 7 mode `$0E` (`7:4000`) |
| `$DA0F-$DA13` | "copied" counter and marker of the album copy screen | bank 4 mode `$0F` |
| `$DA16-$DA30` | main menu cursor, icon animators, transition stamp | bank 7 mode `$00` (`7:71AF`) |
| `$DA31-$DA32` | cursor-at-edge detector of pen and stamp | bank 4 modes `$10`/`$11` |
| `$DA33-$DA43` | owner registration: decorative sprites, on-screen keyboard | bank 9 mode `$08` |
| `$DA44-$DA48` | SHOOT image-effect vector | bank 6 |
| `$DA49-$DA5A` | owner block buffer (SRAM `FB8-FC9`), 18 bytes | banks 2, 7, 9 |
| `$DA5B-$DA90` | tag-head buffer (SRAM tag offsets `F00-F35`), `$36` bytes | banks 0, 2, 9 |
| `$DA91-$DA95` | comment editor state | bank 9, 4, 8 |
| `$DA96-$DAAB` | camera statistics and game records (SRAM `10BB-10D0`), also the bonus-picture lock | banks 0, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 |
| `$DAAC-$DB4C`, `$DBCD` | delete-photo scatter effect | bank 4 mode `$0F` |
| `$DB4D-$DBCA` | print engine tables and state | bank 0 mode `$1D` (+ banks 6, 8) |
| `$DBCB-$DBCF` | shared print/result flags | banks 0, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9 |
| `$DBD0-$DBFE` | printer-link leftovers (write-only / dead path) | bank 0 |


**Bytes of the page with no proven access.** These ranges never appear in `the access summary` and no row above covers them. No proven instruction reads or writes them; an access through HL/DE/BC or a block copy cannot be excluded, so they are **not** claimed to be unused.

| Address | Size | Name | Type/format | Used by (bank:function) | Meaning, values, init | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `$DA0D-$DA0E` | 2 | `gap_da0d` | unknown | no proven access | Between the link-screen variables (`DA00-DA0C`) and the copy-screen variables (`DA0F-DA13`). No proven access, no P row pointing into it. Possibly spare/padding of the allocation; no evidence either way. | ? |
| `$DA14-$DA15` | 2 | `gap_da14` | unknown | no proven access | Between the copy-screen variables and the main-menu variables (`DA16-`). No proven access; `DA16-DA18` are cleared one by one at `00:2EE9-2EEF`, which does not include these two. | ? |
| `$DBD2-$DBEB` | 26 | `gap_dbd2` | unknown | no proven access | After the two write-only printer status bytes (`DBD0/DBD1`) and before the `DBEC` table. No proven access and no P row inside; the `DBEC` table (`9 x u16`, row above) ends at `DBFD`, so this gap is also not part of it. | ? |
